<data_loading_pattern>
`mini_demo_data.json` = curated subset for the demo.
Use this pattern for Colab compatibility (GitHub URL with local fallback):
```python
GITHUB_DATA_URL = "https://raw.githubusercontent.com/ai-inventor-papers/ai-invention-295f8e-prompt-bans-substitute-not-just-subtract/fork/run_jSassd-YWLVC/round-8/evaluation-7/demo/mini_demo_data.json"
import http.client
import json
import urllib.request
from pathlib import Path

def load_data():
    try:
        with urllib.request.urlopen(GITHUB_DATA_URL, timeout=15) as response:
            return json.loads(response.read().decode())
    # Network errors, timeouts, a placeholder URL, bad JSON, a cut-off response.
    except (OSError, ValueError, http.client.HTTPException) as e:
        print("GitHub data unavailable, using local mini_demo_data.json:", e)
    local = Path("mini_demo_data.json")
    if local.exists(): return json.loads(local.read_text())
    raise FileNotFoundError("Could not load mini_demo_data.json")
```
</data_loading_pattern>

take this repo as inspiration: https://github.com/DrejcPesjak/llm-creativity
use one or more open-weight models (minimum 1B params), probably instruction tuned it
and first run behavioral analysis (similar to repo mentioned above), then move to interpretability (can be manifolds, or also other techniques like sae, circuits, nla, j-lens, logit lens...)
the question is: how does "creativity" manifest inside the model, and can we influence it?
(in layman terms, can we make llms give truly creative and novel solutions?)

(you do not need to implement everything bellow, see whats reasonable, interesting and might lead to new insights)
is "temp" really the "llm creativity knob"?  (Astra found "coincidentally" similar direction for navier-stokes solution, as Buckmaster & Levent)
(always same seed)
1 a) does a tight temp , with topk=10 produce 10 reasoning trajectories of 1 idea/solution but same 10 phrasings
b) or does tight temp, with topk=10 produce 10 reasoning trajectories of 10 ideas/solutions with 1 phrasing each
c) and does loosing up temp, make a) more to 2/3/4 ideas each with 10 phrasings ; or make b) keep original 10 ideas, now each with 10 phrasings
(temp is rather a minor part in this idea, put more focus on the following things)
test: "implement path saerch algorithm" -> does llm always design BFS?
2 can context seeding help? prepand "seed=d32qxk4nt43" or "seed=(horse chair roof love)"
hyp: is llm a probablistic model, and thus gives you the average/mean answer? and only context pushes you into a different subspace
 - ie of possible range of answer [0,100] , on direct question llm gives you 50. then with context you move to [80,100] subspace and llm gives you 90
3 so how much does prompt instructions matter? ("Make a new algorithm", "Make a fully novel algorithm never seen before ... 2000 words ..."
-> analyze the internals , all the runs that produced BFS , vs all runs that produced DFS , vs dijkstra ...
  -> analyze with thought anchors, NLA, SAE ...
  -> what is the average answer? plot a "path search algorithm" manifold, color each algorithm centroid by implemented-count (lets say we have 10 algorithms in total of 100 gens)
     -> then prohibit the top 5 in prompt, and see if the implemented count moves to the edge of those 5, or to the opposite side (if its a circle manifold)
  - (manifold lit: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2605.05115 , https://www.goodfire.com/research/manifold-steering ) - geodesic and mds
    - (the reports need to contain actual geometry plots, actual activation space, not some summary bar charts)

Ten lost\nfile names are restored (11 edits), found by a difflib comparison against the record audit's `report_corrected.md`.\n\n## Layout\n\n| Path | What it is |\n|-|-|\n| `eval.py` | entry point; `--stages s0,a,b,c,d,e,f,g,h,i,j,k,apply,audit,out` (or `all`) |\n| `sections/common.py` | shared helpers: Clopper-Pearson, Wilson, exact McNemar, Newcombe, Fisher, average-rank Spearman, bootstraps, correction records, lint |\n| `sections/a_labeller.py` | A1-A3: labeller v2 validation (references, gold set, controls) and the T03 relabel |\n| `sections/judges.py`, `sections/a_adjudicate.py`, `sections/manual_reads.py` | A4: gated two-family judges (cached), adjudicated column, manual reads |\n| `sections/a_recompute.py` | A5: line-for-line port of exp33's P1 / pooled recovery / slot bootstrap / routing, with the placebo |\n| `sections/item_a.py` ... `sections/item_ghijk.py` | one function per review item: traced values plus report edits |\n| `sections/s0_apply.py`, `sections/outputs.py` | S0 (vendoring, hashes, section index, lint before), APPLY (apply test, diff, lint after, placebo), OUT |\n| `labeller_v2/` | copies of the frozen T03 AST/runtime rules with the one added recogniser; `controls.py` holds the five constructed controls |\n| `audit/rederive.py` | independent stdlib re-derivation of every NEW/CORRECTED value, with placebos; writes `audit/rederive_report.json` |\n| `audit/headline_check.py` | short separate re-derivation of the headline numbers through different code paths (regex relabel of the raw code, counting ranks, bisection bound), with placebos; writes `audit/headline_check.json` |\n| `reproducibility.md` | step-by-step reproduction: environment, inputs by artifact id, commands, seeds, hardware, expected numbers |\n| `audit/judge_cache.jsonl`, `audit/judge_gate.json` | every judge response (a rerun is free) and the gate scores |\n| `audit/web/` | raw `fetch_grep` outputs for every positioning row (`run_fetches.sh` refreshes them) |\n| `corrections.json` | 84 edits (old_text, new_text, item, section, line) and the 141-value ledger |\n| `report_corrected.md`, `report.diff` | the corrected report and a unified diff against `inputs/report/current_report.md` |\n| `positioning.json` | the iteration-8 positioning rows with dated evidence and status |\n| `tables/*.csv` | counting_sort_adjudication, relabel_confusion, labeller_v2_validation, recovery_by_source, gemma_natural_variance, control_protocols, search_by_arm, lint |\n| `eval_out.json` (= `full_eval_out.json`), `mini_eval_out.json`, `preview_eval_out.json` | `exp_eval_sol_out` output, schema-validated |\n| `results/` | intermediate JSON for every stage (a2 validation, a3 relabel, a4 adjudication, a5 recompute, items, apply, audit) |\n| `inputs/` | the vendored report, the record audit's files, the T03 bench copy (from exp33's vendored bench), `input_hashes.json`, `resolved_paths.json` |\n\n## How to run\n\n```bash\nuv sync                          # creates .venv from pyproject.toml / uv.lock\nuv run eval.py --stages all      # about 1 minute; judge calls come from audit/judge_cache.jsonl, so a rerun costs nothing\npython3 -I audit/rederive.py     # the stdlib audit on its own\n```\n\nThe iteration-7 dependency workspaces are read in place, read-only, through one constant, `DEP_ROOT`. On the run's\nstorage it is `../../../iter_7/gen_art`; in the published repository it is this folder's parent, where they are\nsibling folders. Set `AII_DEP_ROOT` to override (see `reproducibility.md`). The report and the record audit's files\nare vendored into `inputs/` with sha256 hashes in `inputs/input_hashes.json`.\n\n## Departures and limits\n\n- **Constructed controls.** Two of the ten judge-gate controls, and both positive labeller controls, are dictionary-form\n  counting sorts that I wrote. The bench has no independent exemplar of that form. The other eight gate controls are\n  bench references and gold items, and a dictionary-using full sort serves as a negative control.\n- **The T2 rule.** The T2 (shortest path) rule label was not changed. The adjudication shows the frozen path labeller\n  names degenerate text \"dijkstra\" in 19 pilot programs; none passes.\n- **Placebo (ii).** The pooled point recovery is a ratio of sums and does not depend on slot order (checked). The\n  slot-shuffle placebo therefore acts on the paired bootstrap interval, which changes only slightly because the DAS\n  cells are near-saturated. A label-shuffle placebo is added; it changes the point value.\n- **Not run.** The Gemma dormancy checks (reverse interchange, projected-map retention), the Qwen untrained network on\n  connected components and kth smallest, and a prefill-matched instruction cell are stated as not run. They are left to\n  later artifacts. Nothing else needs restoring. McNemar (binomial on the discordant pairs).\"\"\"\n   185\t    n = b + c\n   186\t    if n == 0:\n   187\t        return 1.0\n   188\t    k = min(b, c)\n   189\t    p = sum(math.comb(n, i) for i in ### [59] TOOL CALL — Write · 2026-10-26 13:22:44 UTC

```
File: /ai-inventor/aii_data/runs/run_jSassd-YWLVC/4_gen_paper_repo/_4_gen_demo_art/notebook_workspaces/iter_8/art_vjtPMtWGMjsB/build_mini_demo_data.py

#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""Build mini_demo_data.json for code_demo.ipynb from the evaluation artifact (art_vjtPMtWGMjsB, gen_art_evaluation_7)
and its exp33 dependency (art_veI9l_Rb9wDY, gen_art_experiment_33).

ONE dataset: the 960 kth-smallest (T03) programs of exp33, grouped into their 82 arm cells (one example per cell).
Every slot carries exp33's stored labels, the runtime labels of the sandboxed tracer (old and v2 rules, from the
artifact's results/a3_relabel.json) and, for the 42 adjudicated programs, the cached judge labels. Program text is
shipped for the 11 cells the demo relabels live (counting sort cells and the placebo cells of the headline check).

Usage:  python3 -I build_mini_demo_data.py --artifact-dir <gen_art_evaluation_7> --dep-dir <iter_7/gen_art>
"""

import argparse
import json
import sys
from collections import Counter
from pathlib import Path

CODE_ARMS = ["FULL_F_counting_sort", "DASLY_F_counting_sort_k1", "DASLY_F_counting_sort_k4", "DASLY_F_counting_sort_k16",
             "F1_counting_sort", "F2_counting_sort", "F3_counting_sort",
             "FULL_F_quickselect", "DASLY_F_quickselect_k1", "BASE_PAD", "BASE_W5"]
RAW_KEYS = ["sample_id", "slot_index", "passed", "valid", "label", "labeller_source", "runtime_label", "ast_label",
            "finish_reason"]


def jl(p):
    return [json.loads(l) for l in Path(p).read_text().splitlines() if l.strip()]


def main():
    ap = argparse.ArgumentParser()
    ap.add_argument("--artifact-dir", required=True)
    ap.add_argument("--dep-dir", required=True, help="folder holding gen_art_experiment_33")
    ap.add_argument("--out", default=str(Path(__file__).resolve().parent / "mini_demo_data.json"))
    a = ap.parse_args()
    art, e33 = Path(a.artifact_dir), Path(a.dep_dir) / "gen_art_experiment_33"
    bench = art / "inputs" / "bench_T03" / "bench" / "T03_kth_smallest"

    rows = [r for r in jl(e33 / "data" / "labels.jsonl") if r["task"] == "T03"]
    a3 = {r["sample_id"]: r for r in json.loads((art / "results" / "a3_relabel.json").read_text())["rows"]}
    a4 = json.loads((art / "results" / "a4_adjudication.json").read_text())
    adj = {r["sample_id"]: r for r in a4["rows"] if r["task"] == "T03"}
    a2 = json.loads((art / "results" / "a2_validation.json").read_text())
    a5 = json.loads((art / "results" / "a5_recompute.json").read_text())

    cells: dict[str, list] = {}
    for r in rows:
        s = {k: r.get(k) for k in RAW_KEYS}
        t = a3[r["sample_id"]]
        s["trace"] = {k: t[k] for k in ("rt_v1", "rt_v2", "ast_v1", "ast_v2")}
        if r["arm"] in CODE_ARMS:
            s["code"] = r.get("code") or ""
        if r["sample_id"] in adj:
            j = adj[r["sample_id"]]
            s["judge"] = {"A": j["judge_A"], "B": j["judge_B"], "in_agreement_sample": j["in_agreement_sample"]}
        cells.setdefault(r["arm"], []).append(s)
    examples = [{"task": "T03", "arm": arm, "n_slots": len(ss), "has_code": arm in CODE_ARMS,
                 "slots": sorted(ss, key=lambda s: s["slot_index"])} for arm, ss in cells.items()]

    # A2 validation inputs: bench references and gold set (program text + stored labels), constructed-control traces
    g_by = {g["gold_id"]: g for g in a2["gold_rows"]}
    refs = [{"ref": p.stem, "code": p.read_text(),
             "trace": {k: next(x for x in a2["ref_rows"] if x["ref"] == p.stem)[k] for k in ("rt_v1", "rt_v2")}}
            for p in sorted((bench / "references").glob("*.py"))]
    gold = [{k: g.get(k) for k in ("gold_id", "source", "runtime", "ast", "adjudicated", "known", "code")} |
            {"trace": {k: g_by[g["gold_id"]][k] for k in ("rt_v1", "rt_v2")}} for g in jl(bench / "gold_set.jsonl")]
    controls = {c["control"]: {"rt_v1": c["rt_v1"], "rt_v2": c["rt_v2"], "sig": c["signature"],
                               "passed": c["passes_hidden_tests"], "n_pass": c["n_pass"], "n_cases": c["n_cases"]}
                for c in a2["controls"]}
    gate_json = json.loads((e33 / "inputs" / "exp11" / "bench" / "T03_kth_smallest" / "gate.json").read_text())["gold"]

    T = json.loads((e33 / "results" / "targets_T03.json").read_text())
    ks = json.loads((e33 / "results" / "kstar_T03.json").read_text())
    site = json.loads((e33 / "results" / "site_T03.json").read_text())
    p1 = json.loads((e33 / "results" / "p1.json").read_text())["tasks"]["T03"]
    na = json.loads((e33 / "results" / "novelty_audit.json").read_text())
    item0a = json.loads((art / "results" / "item_0a.json").read_text())
    hc = json.loads((art / "audit" / "headline_check.json").read_text())
    final = json.loads((art / "results" / "final_labels_T03.json").read_text())

    meta = {
        "description": "Iteration-8 record repair, item 0a (the counting-sort relabel of kth smallest): every exp33 T03 "
                       "program grouped by arm cell, with the inputs that eval.py stage 'a' reads.",
        "source": {"programs": "art_veI9l_Rb9wDY (gen_art_experiment_33): data/labels.jsonl#task==T03",
                   "trace_labels": "art_vjtPMtWGMjsB (gen_art_evaluation_7): results/a3_relabel.json (sandboxed tracer, "
                                   "old and v2 runtime rules)",
                   "judge_labels": "art_vjtPMtWGMjsB: results/a4_adjudication.json (cached judge calls)",
                   "program_text": f"shipped for {len(CODE_ARMS)} cells: " + ", ".join(CODE_ARMS)},
        "read_date": "2026-10-23",
        "n_rows": len(rows), "n_cells": len(examples),
        "exp33": {"targets_T03": {k: T[k] for k in ("task", "default", "kept_Y", "landing")},
                  "kstar_T03": {k: ks[k] for k in ("task", "k_star", "kept_Y_cells")},
                  "site_T03": {k: site[k] for k in ("task", "site", "L_star")},
                  "p1_T03": {"per_k": p1["per_k"]},
                  "novelty_audit": {"rows": [{k: r[k] for k in ("sample_id", "task", "arm", "class")} for r in na["rows"]]},
                  "gate_json_gold": {k: gate_json[k] for k in ("kappa_runtime_ast", "runtime_vs_adjudicated",
                                                               "raw_agreement", "n")}},
        "labeller_validation": {"references": refs, "gold": gold, "control_traces": controls},
        "judge_gate": a4["gate"],
        "expected_from_full_run": {
            "a2": {k: a2[k] for k in ("ref_agreement_v1", "ref_agreement_v2", "gold_new", "controls_pass",
                                      "n_controls", "relabel_allowed")},
            "a3": {k: v for k, v in json.loads((art / "results" / "a3_relabel.json").read_text()).items() if k != "rows"},
            "a4_all_tasks": {k: a4[k] for k in ("kappa", "n_programs", "n_manual_reads", "manual_overrides")},
            "a5": {"placebo_T03": a5["placebo"]["T03"], "new_T03_per_k": a5["new"]["T03"]["per_k"],
                   "routing": a5["routing"], "novelty_audit": a5["novelty_audit"]},
            "final_label_counts": dict(Counter(final.values())),
            "item_0a_values": item0a["values"],
            "headline_H1_H2": [c for c in hc["checks"] if c["name"].startswith(("H1", "H2"))],
        },
    }
    out = {"metadata": meta, "datasets": [{"dataset": "kth_smallest_T03_cells", "examples": examples}]}
    Path(a.out).write_text(json.dumps(out, separators=(",", ":"), ensure_ascii=False))
    print(f"wrote {a.out}: {len(examples)} cells, {len(rows)} programs, "
          f"{sum('code' in s for e in examples for s in e['slots'])} with program text, "
          f"{Path(a.out).stat().st_size / 1024:.0f} KB")

    # check: the shipped AST rules reproduce every stored AST label (v1 = v2 with the iteration-8 recogniser off)
    sys.path[:0] = [str(art), str(art / "inputs" / "bench_T03")]
    from labeller_v2 import ast_rules_v2
    from bench.common.astrules import ast_label as ast_v1
    bad = [s["sample_id"] for e in examples for s in e["slots"] if "code" in s and s["code"] and "def solve" in s["code"]
           and (ast_v1("T03", s["code"]), ast_rules_v2.ast_label("T03", s["code"])) != (s["trace"]["ast_v1"], s["trace"]["ast_v2"])]
    print("AST relabel check on shipped programs:", "OK" if not bad else f"{len(bad)} mismatches {bad[:5]}")


if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```

File: /ai-inventor/aii_data/runs/run_jSassd-YWLVC/4_gen_paper_repo/_4_gen_demo_art/notebook_workspaces/iter_8/art_vjtPMtWGMjsB/build_mini_demo_data.py
OLD: def jl(p):
    return [json.loads(l) for l in Path(p).read_text().splitlines() if l.strip()]
NEW: def jl(p):
    return [json.loads(l) for l in Path(p).read_text().splitlines() if l.strip()]


def kappa(a, b):
    """Cohen's kappa, as sections/common.py cohen_kappa."""
    n = len(a)
    po = sum(x == y for x, y in zip(a, b)) / n
    ca, cb = Counter(a), Counter(b)
    pe = sum(ca[k] * cb.get(k, 0) for k in ca) / (n * n)
    return (1.0 if po == 1.0 else 0.0) if pe >= 1.0 else (po - pe) / (1 - pe)


def a4_t03_summary(rows):
    """The full run's adjudication restricted to its kth-smallest programs (what the demo can recompute)."""
    v = [r for r in rows if r["valid"] and r["judge_A"] and r["judge_B"]]
    final = {}
    for r in rows:
        final.setdefault(r["cell"], Counter())[r["final_label"]] += 1
    return {"n_programs": len(rows), "n_distinct": len({r["code_sha256"] for r in rows if r["valid"]}),
            "kappa": {"rule_vs_A": kappa([r["rule_label"] for r in v], [r["judge_A"] for r in v]),
                      "rule_vs_B": kappa([r["rule_label"] for r in v], [r["judge_B"] for r in v]),
                      "A_vs_B": kappa([r["judge_A"] for r in v], [r["judge_B"] for r in v]), "n": len(v)},
            "n_manual_reads": len({r["code_sha256"] for r in rows if r["valid"] and r["manual_read"]}),
            "manual_overrides": sum(bool(r["manual_overrides_rule"]) for r in rows),
            "final_counts": {c: dict(n) for c, n in final.items()}}

The research question is how algorithm choice, a proxy for "creativity", is represented inside an LLM (Qwen3-8B writing Python programs). It also asks whether prompt bans, context, or activation edits (DAS subspaces, i.e. learned low-rank directions in the residual stream) can move that choice. Step 0 is an **erratum and re-derivation pass** over the iteration-7 report. It answers all 11 items (0a–0k) of a blocking review. Every corrected number is traced to a raw file (`corrections.json`: recorded value, recomputed value, status), and the report is patched with 84 machine-checked edits.\n\n**What this notebook runs:** the stage that carries the headline correction, **item 0a**, on the real data (all 960 kth-smallest programs of exp33):\n\n1. **A1–A2. Extend and validate the labeller.** The frozen kth-smallest labeller sent *counting sort in dictionary form* (`count = {}` … `for v in sorted(count): k -= count[v]`) to `other`. One AST recogniser is added, and it is validated **before** any relabel: 6 bench references, the 28-program gold set (kappa must not drop), and 5 constructed controls.\n2. **A3. Relabel** all 960 kth-smallest programs: 35 transitions, all `other → counting_sort`.\n3. **A4. Adjudicate** the 42 kth-smallest programs in cells with more than 20% `other`: rule label, two gated LLM judges (labels cached in the data), and manual reads where they disagree.\n4. **A5. Recompute** exp33\'s *pooled recovery* (the share of the full-state activation-swap effect that a rank-k DAS subspace edit recovers) with exp33\'s own slot bootstrap. A placebo comes first: under the old labels the port must reproduce exp33\'s published numbers.\n5. **Item 0a**: the value ledger (recorded vs recomputed: MATCH / CORRECTED / NEW) and the report edits. Then the independent **headline check** (a regex relabel of the raw program text).\n\n**Headline:** pooled kth-smallest recovery falls from **1.59 / 1.57 / 1.55** to **1.01 / 1.00 / 0.99** at DAS ranks 1 / 4 / 16. The iteration-7 claim that a subspace edit "recovers 1.6× the full-state effect" was a labelling artefact.\n\n| `eval.py` stage | what it does | in this notebook |\n|-|-|-|\n| `s0` | vendors the 1.3 MB iteration-7 report, hashes every input, lints the report | not run (the report is not shipped) |\n| `a` | A1–A5 above | **run** |\n| `b` … `k` | items 0a–0k: traced values + report edits | **item 0a run**; 0b–0k read six other iteration-7 artifacts |\n| `apply` | applies all 84 edits, writes the diff, lint after, placebo | not run (needs every item and the report) |\n| `audit` | independent re-derivations (`audit/rederive.py`, `audit/headline_check.py`) | **headline checks H1–H2 run** |\n| `out` | writes `eval_out.json` | replaced by the summary cells at the end |\n\nCPU only, well under a minute, no API calls.'}

They are grouped into their **82 arm cells**, one example per cell, with 30 (or 4) slots each. The slots are CRN-matched: slot *i* uses the same random seed in every cell. Arm names:\n\n- `BASE_*`: unedited generation (`BASE_W5`: the ban prompt; `BASE_PAD`: the padded twin of a forcing prompt);\n- `FULL_*`: the **full-state exchange**, where the whole residual stream at layer 16 is swapped in from a source prompt;\n- `DASLY_*_k{r}`: the **rank-r DAS subspace** edit; `RAND_*` and `PERMLY_*` are its random-subspace and permuted-label controls;\n- the source is either the ban prompt (`ban`) or a prompt that forces one algorithm (`F_quickselect`, `F_counting_sort`).\n\nEach slot carries exp33's stored fields: `label`, `passed` (hidden tests), `labeller_source`, and the stored `runtime_label` / `ast_label`. `trace` holds the runtime labels of the sandboxed tracer, which runs each program on probe inputs, under the old and the v2 rules. The tracer itself is not shipped. The 42 adjudicated programs also carry the cached labels of judges A and B.\n\n**Program text** is shipped for the 11 cells this notebook relabels live (the counting-sort cells and the placebo cells of the headline check). The metadata holds exp33's kth-smallest configuration, its published pooled recovery (for the placebo), and the labeller-validation inputs. It also holds the numbers the full run produced, for the comparison at the end."}
```

betainc(a: float, b: float, x: float) -> float:\n    """Regularised incomplete beta I_x(a, b)."""\n    if x <= 0.0:\n        return 0.0\n    if x >= 1.0:\n        return 1.0\n    lbt = math.lgamma(a + b) - math.lgamma(a) - math.lgamma(b) + a * math.log(x) + b * math.log(1.0 - x)\n    bt = math.exp(lbt)\n    if x < (a + 1.0) / (a + b + 2.0):\n        return bt * _betacf(a, b, x) / a\n    return 1.0 cohen_kappa(a: list[str], b: list[str]) -> float:\n    n = len(a)\n    if n == 0:\n        return float("nan")\n    po = sum(x == y for x, y in zip(a, b)) / n\n    ca, cb = Counter(a), Counter(b)\n    pe = sum(ca[k] * cb.get(k, 0) for k in ca) / (n * n)\n    if pe >= 1.0:\n        return 1.0 if po == 1.0 else 0.0\n    return (po - pe) / (1 - pe)\n\n\ndef fmt(x: float | None, nd: int = 2) -> str:\n A program is labelled `counting_sort` when it does all of the following:\n- builds a frequency map over the input values (`Counter(nums)`, `defaultdict(int)`, or `{}` filled with `m[x] = m.get(x, 0) + 1`);\n- walks the map in sorted key order, accumulating counts until rank k (or expanding value × count into a list);\n- never sorts the whole input and never uses a heap.\n\nThe frozen rules sent these programs to `unknown` (AST) and `other` (runtime). Here is a typical program from the full-state exchange of the forced counting-sort source (slot 26):\n\n```python\ndef solve(nums, k):\n    count = {}\n    for num in nums:\n        count[num] = count.get(num, 0) + 1\n    sorted_nums = sorted(count.keys())\n    for num in sorted_nums:\n        if k <= count[num]:\n            return num\n        k -= count[num]\n    return -1\n```'}

sort of the whole input list and no heap."""\n    maps = _freq_maps(f)\n    if not maps or _heapish(f) or "solve" not in f.funcs:\n        return False\n    args = f.funcs["solve"].args.args\n    inp = args[0].arg if args else "nums"\n    sorts_map = False\n    for node in ast.walk(f.tree):\n        if not isinstance(node, ast.Call):\n            continue\n        fn = node.func\n        if isinstance(fn, ast.Attribute) and fn.attr == "sort" and isinstance(fn.value, ast.Name) and fn.value.id == inp:\n            return False  # sorts the whole input list\n        if isinstance(fn, ast.Name) and fn.id == "sorted" and node.args:\n            a0 = node.args[0]\n            if isinstance(a0, ast.Name) and a0.id == inp:\n                return False  # sorted(<input>)\n            if any(kw.arg == "key" for kw in node.keywords):\n                kv = ast.unparse([kw for kw in node.keywords if kw.arg == "key"][0].value)\n                if "[1]" in kv or "get" in kv:\n                    continue  # sorted by count, not by value\n            if isinstance(a0, ast.Name) and a0.id in maps:\n                sorts_map = True\n            elif isinstance(a0, ast.Call) and isinstance(a0.func, ast.Attribute) and a0.func.attr in ("keys", "items") \\\n                    and isinstance(a0.func.value, ast.Name) and a0.func.value.id in maps:\n                sorts_map = True\n            elif isinstance(a0, ast.Call) and isinstance(a0.func, ast.Name) and a0.func.id == "set" and a0.args and \\\n                    isinstance(a0.args[0], ast.Name) and a0.args[0].id == inp:\n                sorts_map = True  # sorted distinct values walked against the frequency map\n    if not sorts_map:\n        return False\n    walks = f.rx(r"\\bk\\s*-=") or f.rx(r"(>=|<=|<|>)\\s*k\\b") or f.rx(r"\\bk\\s*(>=|<=|<|>)") or \\\n        f.rx(r"\\[\\s*\\w+\\s*\\]\\s*\\*\\s*\\w+") or f.rx(r"\\.extend\\(") or f.rx(r"\\[\\s*k\\s*-\\s*1\\s*\\]")\n    return bool(walks)'}
```

{'notebook_path': '/ai-inventor/aii_data/runs/run_jSassd-YWLVC/4_gen_paper_repo/_4_gen_demo_art/notebook_workspaces/iter_8/art_vjtPMtWGMjsB/code_demo.ipynb', 'cell_id': 'c16', 'new_source': '### The iteration-8 extension: counting sort in dictionary form\n\nThis is the one recogniser added in iteration 8, with its helper. A program is labelled `counting_sort` when it does all of the following:\n- builds a frequency map over the input values (`Counter(nums)`, `defaultdict(int)`, or `{}` filled with `m[x] = m.get(x, 0) + 1`);\n- walks the map in sorted key order, accumulating counts until rank k (or expanding value × count into a list);\n- never sorts the whole input and never uses a heap.\n\nThe frozen rules sent these programs to `unknown` (AST) and `other` (runtime). In the full-state exchange of the forced counting-sort source, 23 of the 30 slots wrote exactly this program:\n\n```python\ndef solve(nums, k):\n    count = {}\n    for num in nums:\n        count[num] = count.get(num, 0) + 1\n    sorted_nums = sorted(count.keys())\n    for num in sorted_nums:\n        if k <= count[num]:\n            return num\n        k -= count[num]\n    return -1\n```'}
````

A *source* is a prompt pair: a base prompt and a source prompt that drives the model to a target algorithm set *Y*. The **full-state exchange** swaps the whole layer-16 residual stream from the source run into the base run; the **DAS edit** swaps only a learned rank-k subspace. A cell's count is the number of its 30 slots whose program **passes the hidden tests and is labelled in Y**:\n\n$$\\text{pooled recovery}(k) \\;=\\; \\frac{\\sum_{s}\\big(\\#\\mathrm{DAS}_k(s) - \\#\\mathrm{base}(s)\\big)}{\\sum_{s}\\big(\\#\\mathrm{full}(s) - \\#\\mathrm{base}(s)\\big)}$$\n\n1.0 means the subspace edit carries the whole effect of the full-state swap. The 95% interval resamples the 30 CRN **slots** jointly in every cell, using `numpy.default_rng(20261022)` and `BOOT` resamples.\n\nKth smallest has three sources: the ban prompt (Y = {quickselect, size_k_heap}), and the forcing prompts for quickselect and for counting sort. Under the old labels, the full-state exchange of the forced counting-sort source scored **0 of 30**, because its 29 dictionary-form counting sorts were `other`. That shrank the denominator to 51 and inflated recovery to 81/51 = 1.59. The **placebo** runs first: the port under the old labels must reproduce exp33's published numbers."}

dict[str, Any] | None, Y: list[str]) -> int:\n    return int(bool(r) and bool(r.get("passed")) and r.get("label") in Y)\n\n\ndef vec(A: dict, arm: str | None, Y: list[str]) -> np.ndarray | None:\n    if not arm or arm not in A:\n        return None\n    return np.array([cy(A[arm].get(s), Y) for s in range(N)])\n\n\ndef pass_rate(A: dict, arm: str) -> float | None:\n    if arm not in A:\n        return None\n    rs = [A[arm].get(s) for s in range(N) if A[arm].get(s)]\n    return float(np.mean([bool(r.get("passed")) for r in rs])) if rs else None\n\n\ndef sources(task: str) -> list[dict[str, Any]]:\n    # original: exp33 results/targets_{task}.json, kstar_{task}.json and site_{task}.json\n    T = META["exp33"][f"targets_{task}"]\n    ks = META["exp33"][f"kstar_{task}"]\n    site = META["exp33"][f"site_{task}"]\n    full = "FULL_ban_band" if site["site"] == "band" else f"FULL_ban_L{site[\'L_star\']}"\n    landing = T["landing"] or T["kept_Y"][:1]\n    k = ks["k_star"]\n    out = [{"name": "ban", "Y": landing, "base": "BASE_W5", "full": full,\n            "das": {kk: f"DASLY_ban_k{kk}" for kk in RANKS},\n            "rand": {16: ["RAND_ban_k16_d1", "RAND_ban_k16_d2", "RAND_ban_k16_d3"],\n                     k: ([f"RAND_ban_k{k}"] if k != 16 else ["RAND_ban_k16_d1", "RAND_ban_k16_d2", "RAND_ban_k16_d3"])},\n            "perm": {16: "PERMLY_ban_k16", k: f"PERMLY_ban_k{k}"}}]\n    for Y in ks["kept_Y_cells"]:\n        out.append({"name": f"F_{Y}", "Y": [Y], "base": "BASE_PAD", "full": f"FULL_F_{Y}",\n                    "das": {kk: f"DASLY_F_{Y}_k{kk}" for kk in (1, 4, 16)}, "rand": {16: [f"RAND_F_{Y}_k16"]},\n                    "perm": {16: f"PERMLY_F_{Y}_k16"}})\n    return out\n\n\ndef pooled_recovery(A: dict, srcs: list[dict[str, Any]], k: int, slots: np.ndarray | None = None,\n                    which: str = "das") -> tuple[float | None, float, float]:\n    num = den = 0.0\n    for s in srcs:\n        b, f = vec(A, s["base"], s["Y"]), vec(A, s["full"], s["Y"])\n        arms = ([s["das"].get(k)] if s["das"].get(k) else []) if which == "das" else s["rand"].get(k, [])\n        vs = [v for v in (vec(A, a, s["Y"]) for a in arms) if v is not None]\n        if b is None or f is None or not vs:\n            continue\n        idx = slots if slots is not None else np.arange(N)\n        x = float(np.mean([v[idx].sum() for v in vs]))\n        num += x - b[idx].sum()\n        den += f[idx].sum() - b[idx].sum()\n    return (num / den if den > 0 else None), num, den\n\n\ndef boot_ci(A: dict, srcs: list[dict[str, Any]], k: int, which: str = "das", seed: int = SEED,\n            n_boot: int = BOOT) -> list[float | None]:\n    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)\n    vals = []\n    for _ in range(n_boot):\n        idx = rng.integers(0, N, N)\n        r, _, _ = pooled_recovery(A, srcs, k, idx, which)\n        if r is not None:\n            vals.append(r)\n    if len(vals) < 100:\n        return [None, None]\n    return [float(np.percentile(vals, 2.5)), float(np.percentile(vals, 97.5))]'}

b.sum()) / N),\n                                 "relative_to_full": float((v.sum() - b.sum()) / (f.sum() - b.sum()))\n                                 if f.sum() > b.sum() else None}\n        out["per_source"][s["name"]] = ps\n    cand = sorted({16, ks})\n    for k in RANKS:\n        r, num, den = pooled_recovery(A, srcs, k)\n        rr = pooled_recovery(A, srcs, k, which="rand")[0] if k in cand else None\n        e = {"recovery": r, "num": num, "den": den, "recovery_ci": boot_ci(A, srcs, k) if r is not None else None,\n             "random_recovery": rr}\n        if k in cand:\n            perm = []\n            for s in srcs:\n                a = s["perm"].get(k)\n                b, v = vec(A, s["base"], s["Y"]), vec(A, a, s["Y"])\n                if v is not None and b is not None:\n                    perm.append(float((v.sum() - len(corr)}\n\n\ndef run(final_map: dict[str, str]) -> dict[str, Any]:\n    """final_map: sample_id -> new label for every T03 row (rule_v2, or the adjudicated label where adjudicated)."""\n    rows = load_rows()\n    p1 = {"T03": META["exp33"]["p1_T03"]}  # original: read_json(dep("exp33", "results/p1.json"))["tasks"]\n    res: dict[str, Any] = {"placebo": {}, "old": {}, "new": {}}\n    # placebo 1: the port under OLD labels reproduces exp33\'s per_k (point and CI). {'notebook_path': '/ai-inventor/aii_data/runs/run_jSassd-YWLVC/4_gen_paper_repo/_4_gen_demo_art/notebook_workspaces/iter_8/art_vjtPMtWGMjsB/code_demo.ipynb', 'cell_id': 'c31', 'new_source': 'def stage_a() -> dict[str, Any]:\n    # original: from sections import a_adjudicate as AD, a_labeller as AL, a_recompute as RC, manual_reads as MR\n    base = AL.run()\n    if not base["validation"]["relabel_allowed"]:\n        raise RuntimeError("A2 validation failed: the relabel is blocked")\n    rel = base["relabel"]["rows"]\n    hashes = sorted({r["code_sha256"] for r in AD.programs(base["cells"], rel)})\n    adj = AD.run(base["cells"], rel, MR.resolve(hashes))\n    if adj["missing_manual_codes"] or adj["missing_sample_codes"]:\n        raise RuntimeError(f"manual reads missing for {len(adj[\'missing_manual_codes\'])} + {len(adj[\'missing_sample_codes\'])} programs")\n    fmap = {r["sample_id"]: r["rule_v2"] for r in rel}\n    for r in adj["rows"]:\n        if r["task"] == "T03":\n            fmap[r["sample_id"]] = r["final_label"]\n    write_json(RESULTS / "final_labels_T03.json", fmap)\n    rc = RC.run(fmap)\n    for t, v in rc["placebo"].items():\n        if not v["all_match"]:\n            raise RuntimeError(f"A5 placebo failed on {t}: the port does not reproduce exp33\'s pooled recovery")\n    write_json(RESULTS / "a5_recompute.json", rc)\n    return {"a2": base["validation"], "a3": base["relabel"], "a4": adj, "a5": rc}\n\n\ndef load_ctx() -> dict[str, Any]:\n    return {"a2": read_json(RESULTS / "a2_validation.json"), "a3": read_json(RESULTS / "a3_relabel.json"),\n            "a4": read_json(RESULTS / "a4_adjudication.json"), "a5": _int_keys(read_json(RESULTS / "a5_recompute.json"))}\n\n\ndef _int_keys(o: Any) -> Any:\n    """JSON turns the per_k / das rank keys into strings; restore ints where a key is a rank."""\n    if isinstance(o, dict):\n        return {(int(k) if isinstance(k, str) and k.isdigit() else k): _int_keys(v) for k, v in o.items()}\n    if isinstance(o, list):\n        return [_int_keys(x) for x in o]\n    return o'}

{'notebook_path': '/ai-inventor/aii_data/runs/run_jSassd-YWLVC/4_gen_paper_repo/_4_gen_demo_art/notebook_workspaces/iter_8/art_vjtPMtWGMjsB/code_demo.ipynb', 'cell_id': 'c35', 'new_source': 'def run_items(stages: list[str], ctx: dict[str, Any]) -> dict[str, Any]:\n    # original: from sections import item_a, item_b, item_cde, item_f, item_ghijk, and fn maps all eleven items a-k;\n    # items 0b-0k read six more iteration-7 artifacts (exp34-exp38, eval6), which the demo data does not carry\n    fn = {"a": item_a_run}\n    out = {}\n    for s in stages:\n        if s in fn:\n            t0 = time.time()\n            n0 = len(VALUES)\n            r = fn[s](ctx)\n            r["values"] = VALUES[n0:]\n            out[ITEM_STAGES[s]] = r\n            write_json(RESULTS / f"item_{ITEM_STAGES[s]}.json", r)\n            logger.info(f"item {ITEM_STAGES[s]}: {len(r[\'edits\'])} edits, {len(r[\'values\'])} values ({time.time() - t0:.1f}s)")\n    return out\n\n\ndef write_corrections(items: dict[str, Any], s0: dict[str, Any], ap: dict[str, Any]) -> dict[str, Any]:\n    edits = [e for it in items.values() for e in it["edits"]]\n    vals = [v for it in items.values() for v in it["values"]]\n    by_status = {s: sum(v["status"] == s for v in vals) for s in ("MATCH", "CORRECTED", "NEW", "CARRIED")}\n    corr = {"read_date": READ_DATE, "report_sha256": s0["hashes"]["report"]["sha256"] if s0 else None,\n            "schema": "mirrors art_LzP2qPwUrANz corrections.json: one record per edit (old_text occurs once in the vendored report) plus a value ledger",\n            "n_edits": len(edits), "n_values": len(vals), "values_by_status": by_status,\n            "n_edits_by_item": {k: len(v["edits"]) for k, v in items.items()},\n            "apply": {k: ap.get(k) for k in ("apply_test_pass", "n_edits", "lint_before", "lint_after", "placebo_mutation_fails", "problems")},\n            "corrections": edits, "values": vals}\n    write_json(ROOT / "corrections.json", corr)\n    return corr'}

The demo runs the first two headline checks of `audit/headline_check.py`, wrapped in a function so the script's globals stay local.\n\nThey deliberately take a **different code path**. Each placebo must fail:\n- the old labels must still give 0/30 and 1.59;\n- the regex must find no dictionary counting sort in the quickselect and base cells;\n- shuffled labels must not give 1.0125.\n\nH3–H6 read exp34, exp37, the report and the judge cache, so they are not run here."}

{'notebook_path': '/ai-inventor/aii_data/runs/run_jSassd-YWLVC/4_gen_paper_repo/_4_gen_demo_art/notebook_workspaces/iter_8/art_vjtPMtWGMjsB/code_demo.ipynb', 'cell_id': 'c37', 'new_source': 'def headline_check() -> dict[str, Any]:\n    """audit/headline_check.py, checks H1-H2 and their placebos (the script\'s module-level code, wrapped in a function):\n      H1  full-state exchange of the forced counting-sort source: 29/30 counting sort. Relabel by a plain regex on the raw\n          program text of exp33 data/labels.jsonl (no tracer, no AST rules, no judges, no adjudication table).\n      H2  kth-smallest pooled recovery 1.0125 / 1.0000 / 0.9875 (ranks 1/4/16) from the regex labels, by direct counting."""\n    OUT, PLC = [], []\n\n    def rec(name, got, want, tol=1e-9):\n        ok = abs(got - want) <= tol if isinstance(want, (int, float)) and not isinstance(want, bool) else got == want\n        OUT.append({"name": name, "independent": got, "expected": want, "ok": bool(ok)})\n\n    DICT = re.compile(r"Counter\\(|defaultdict\\(int\\)|=\\s*\\{\\}|dict\\(\\)")\n    SORTKEYS = re.compile(r"sorted\\(\\s*\\w+\\s*(\\.keys\\(\\)|\\.items\\(\\))?\\s*\\)")\n    NOT_IT = re.compile(r"sorted\\(\\s*nums\\s*\\)|nums\\.sort\\(|heap|nsmallest|nlargest")\n\n    def dict_counting(code):\n        return bool(DICT.search(code) and SORTKEYS.search(code) and not NOT_IT.search(code))\n\n    rows = T03_ROWS  # original: every row of exp33 data/labels.jsonl\n    t03 = [r for r in rows if r["task"] == "T03"]\n    lab = {r["sample_id"]: ("counting_sort" if r["label"] == "other" and dict_counting(r.get("code") or "") else r["label"]) for r in t03}\n    cell = lambda arm, y, L: sum(1 for r in t03 if r["arm"] == arm and r.get("passed") and L[r["sample_id"]] in y)\n    full = cell("FULL_F_counting_sort", {"counting_sort"}, lab)\n    rec("H1 FULL_F_counting_sort counting sort (regex relabel of raw code)", full, 29)\n    SRC = [({"quickselect", "size_k_heap"}, "BASE_W5", "FULL_ban_L16", "DASLY_ban_k"), ({"quickselect"}, "BASE_PAD", "FULL_F_quickselect", "DASLY_F_quickselect_k"),\n           ({"counting_sort"}, "BASE_PAD", "FULL_F_counting_sort", "DASLY_F_counting_sort_k")]\n\n    def pooled(k, L):\n        num = sum(cell(f"{d}{k}", y, L) - cell(b, y, L) for y, b, f, d in SRC)\n        den = sum(cell(f, y, L) - cell(b, y, L) for y, b, f, d in SRC)\n        return num / den\n\n    for k, want in ((1, 81 / 80), (4, 80 / 80), (16, 79 / 80)):\n        rec(f"H2 kth pooled recovery rank {k}", pooled(k, lab), want)\n    old = {r["sample_id"]: r["label"] for r in t03}\n    PLC.append({"name": "H1/H2 old labels give 0/30 and 1.59", "failed_as_required": cell("FULL_F_counting_sort", {"counting_sort"}, old) == 0 and round(pooled(1, old), 2) == 1.59})\n    qs = sum(dict_counting(r.get("code") or "") for r in t03 if r["arm"] in ("FULL_F_quickselect", "DASLY_F_quickselect_k1", "BASE_PAD", "BASE_W5"))\n    PLC.append({"name": "regex detector finds no dictionary counting sort in quickselect/base cells", "failed_as_required": qs == 0, "detail": qs})\n    sh = list(lab.values())\n    random.Random(1).shuffle(sh)\n    lab_sh = dict(zip(lab.keys(), sh))\n    try:\n        psh = pooled(1, lab_sh)\n    except ZeroDivisionError:\n        psh = None\n    PLC.append({"name": "shuffled labels do not give 1.0125", "failed_as_required": psh is None or abs(psh - 81 / 80) > 0.05, "detail": psh})\n    res = {"checks": OUT, "placebos": PLC, "n_ok": sum(o["ok"] for o in OUT), "n": len(OUT),\n           "placebos_failed": sum(p["failed_as_required"] for p in PLC), "n_placebos": len(PLC)}\n    write_json(AUDIT / "headline_check.json", res)  # original: (ROOT / "audit" / "headline_check.json").write_text(...)\n    for o in OUT:\n        print(("OK  " if o["ok"] else "FAIL"), o["name"], o["independent"])\n    for p in PLC:\n        print("PLACEBO", "fails as required" if p["failed_as_required"] else "DID NOT FAIL", p["name"], p.get("detail", ""))\n    print(f"{res[\'n_ok\']}/{res[\'n\']} headline numbers re-derived; {res[\'placebos_failed\']}/{res[\'n_placebos\']} placebos failed as required")\n    return res\n\n\ndef stage_audit() -> dict[str, Any]:\n    """The original runs audit/rederive.py and audit/headline_check.py in subprocesses and merges their reports; the\n    demo runs headline checks H1-H2 in-process."""\n    return {"headline": headline_check()}'}
```

The denominator of the pooled ratio rises from 51 to 80, and kth-smallest recovery falls from about 1.6 to about 1.0. A rank-1 DAS subspace therefore carries roughly the whole effect of the full-state swap, not 1.6 times it. Figure (f) adds the routing correction: on kth smallest the subspace edit changes the *form* of the counting sort (an array of counts instead of a dictionary), not the algorithm. The labeller was validated before the relabel (references 6/6, gold kappa unchanged at 0.951, controls 5/5). The headline numbers are re-derived through an independent regex path, whose placebos fail as required.\n- **Not run in this notebook.** Items 0b–0k, `s0`, `apply`, and the `audit/rederive.py` re-derivation read six more iteration-7 artifacts and the 1.3 MB report. The full run (`uv sync && uv run eval.py --stages all`, about 25 s with the judge cache present) applies all 84 report edits and cuts the residual-claim lint from 35 hits to 0. Its stdlib audit re-derives 97 values with 0 mismatches, with 6/6 placebos failing as required.\n- **Not run in the original either** (stated in its record): the Gemma dormancy checks, the Qwen untrained network beyond shortest path, and a prefill-matched instruction cell.\n- **Scale.** The only tunable is `BOOT`. 2000 is the original setting, and the whole notebook runs in well under a minute on a CPU.'}

arm, ["counting_sort"]) for arm, _, A in rows_c])\nax.imshow(grid, cmap="Greens", aspect="auto", vmin=0, vmax=1.25)\nax.set_yticks(range(len(rows_c)), [f"{name}  ({int(g.sum())}/30)" for (_, name, _), g in zip(rows_c, grid)], fontsize=8)\nax.set_xticks(range(0, N, 5))\nax.set_xticks(np.arange(-0.5, N, 1), minor=True)\nax.set_yticks(np.arange(-0.5, len(rows_c), 1), minor=True)\nax.grid(which="minor", color="white", lw=0.8)\nax.tick_params(which="minor", length=0)\nax.set_xlabel("CRN slot")\nax.set_title("(c) Slot level: program passes and is a counting sort")\n\n# (d) bootstrap distribution of the rank-1 pooled recovery (the same resamples as boot_ci)\nax = axs[1, 0]\nsrcs = sources("T03")\nfor name, A, col in (("old labels", A_old, "tab:red"), ("corrected labels", A_new, "tab:blue")):\n    rng = np.random.default_rng(SEED)\n    vals = []\n    for _ in range(BOOT):\n        r, _, _ = pooled_recovery(A, srcs, 1, rng.integers(0, N, N), "das")\n        if r is not None:\n            vals.append(r)\n    ax.hist(vals, bins=np.linspace(0.85, 1.85, 81), alpha=0.6, color=col, label=f"{name}: 95% [{np.percentile(vals, 2.5):.2f}, a5["new"]["T03"]["per_source"]\nsrc_names = list(ps_new)\nbars = [("base", lambda s: ps_new[s]["base_count"]), ("full, old labels", lambda s: ps_old[s]["full_count"]),\n        ("full, corrected", lambda s: ps_new[s]["full_count"])] + \\\n       [(f"DAS k{k}", (lambda k: lambda s: ps_new[s]["das"].get(k, {}).get("count", 0))(k)) for k in (1, 4, 16)]\nwb = 0.13\nfor i, (bn, get) in enumerate(bars):\n    hs = [get(s) for s in src_names]\n    ax.bar(np.arange(len(src_names)) + (i - 2.5) * wb, hs, width=wb, label=bn)\n    if bn == "full, old labels":  # mark the zero that drove the old ratio\n        for j, hv in enumerate(hs):\n            if hv == 0:\n                ax.annotate("0", (j + (i - 2.5) * wb, 0), xytext=(0, 2), textcoords="offset points", ha="center", fontsize=8, color="tab:orange")\nax.set_xticks(np.arange(len(src_names)), [f"{s}\\nY = {\'+\'.join(ps_new[s][\'Y\'])}" for s in src_names], fontsize=8)\nax.set_ylim(0, 37)\nax.set_ylabel("correct programs labelled in Y (of 30)")\nax.set_title("(e) Counts behind the pooled ratio, by source")\nax.legend(fontsize=7, ncol=3, loc="upper center")\n\n# (f) implementation form of the counting sorts (regex on "DASLY_F_counting_sort_k16"]\nbottom = np.zeros(len(arms_f))\nfor c in ("array", "dict_sorted_keys", "dict_value_range", "neither"):\n    h = np.array([forms[a].get(c, 0) for a in arms_f])\n    ax.bar(range(len(arms_f)), h, bottom=bottom, label=c)\n    bottom += h\nax.set_xticks(range(len(arms_f)), ["full state", "DAS k1", "DAS k4", "DAS k16"])\nax.set_ylim(0, 37)\nax.set_ylabel("programs ### [277] TOOL CALL — Write · 2026-10-26 13:44:00 UTC

````
File: /ai-inventor/aii_data/runs/run_jSassd-YWLVC/4_gen_paper_repo/_4_gen_demo_art/notebook_workspaces/iter_8/art_vjtPMtWGMjsB/README.md

# Demo: fixing errors in the iteration-7 research record (item 0a, the counting-sort relabel)

A runnable notebook version of `eval.py` from the evaluation artifact `art_vjtPMtWGMjsB` (iteration 8, Step 0: an
erratum and re-derivation pass over the iteration-7 report). The notebook runs the stage that carries the headline
correction on the real data: all 960 kth-smallest programs that Qwen3-8B wrote in exp33.

1. Validate the labeller extension (counting sort in dictionary form) before any relabel.
2. Relabel the 960 programs.
3. Adjudicate the high-`other` cells, using the cached judge labels and the manual reads.
4. Recompute exp33's pooled recovery with exp33's own slot bootstrap.
5. Build the item 0a value ledger and report edits, then run the independent regex headline check.

**Result (`BOOT = 2000`, the original setting):** every quantity the notebook recomputes equals the full run, 24 of 24
rows in the notebook's comparison table. The placebo reproduces exp33's published pooled recovery and intervals
exactly; 1.59 / 1.57 / 1.55 at DAS ranks 1 / 4 / 16 become the corrected **1.0125 [0.952, 1.079] / 1.000 [0.926, 1.075] /
0.9875 [0.918, 1.065]**. The 16 report edits and 22 ledger values the notebook generates match the original run's
`results/item_0a.json` exactly. The original has one more edit with two values, the shortest-path pilot, which needs
data the demo does not carry.

## Layout

| Path | What it is |
|-|-|
| `code_demo.ipynb` | the notebook: `eval.py` and the section modules stage `a` calls, split into cells with explanations; saved with its outputs (comparison table and a 6-panel figure) |
| `mini_demo_data.json` | the demo data, one dataset `kth_smallest_T03_cells`: 82 exp33 arm cells (960 programs, program text for the 252 programs of the 11 cells relabelled live); the metadata holds exp33's kth-smallest config, its published pooled recovery, the labeller-validation inputs and the full run's numbers for comparison |
| `build_mini_demo_data.py` | how `mini_demo_data.json` was built (stdlib only); it also checks that the shipped AST rules reproduce every stored AST label |
| `.aii/manifest.yaml` | storage decisions (none needed: nothing here is heavy) |

The notebook loads `mini_demo_data.json` from this repository's raw GitHub URL and falls back to the local copy.

## How to run

**Colab:** open `code_demo.ipynb` and run all cells. The first cell installs `loguru` and nothing else; Colab already
has numpy and matplotlib. Runtime is about 10 s on a CPU.

**Locally** (Python 3.12):

```bash
uv venv --seed --python 3.12 .nbenv
uv pip install --python .nbenv/bin/python jupyter ipykernel
.nbenv/bin/jupyter nbconvert --to notebook --execute code_demo.ipynb --output code_demo.ipynb
```

Off Colab, the install cell pins numpy 2.0.2 and matplotlib 3.10.0, Colab's versions. Running the notebook writes the
same intermediate files as the original into `eval_demo_run/`: `results/*.json`, `tables/*.csv`, `audit/*.json`,
`corrections.json` and `logs/run.log`.

**Configuration** (config cell):
- `BOOT`: the number of slot-bootstrap resamples, 2000 as in exp33. The placebo matches exp33's intervals exactly only at 2000; with fewer it compares the point estimates.
- `STAGES`: `a,audit`.

Scale-up record, clean-venv runs including package installs: `BOOT` = 100 took 10 s, 500 took 9 s, 2000 took 10–11 s.

## What differs from the original `eval.py`

The code is the original code, split into cells. Only these changes were made, each marked in the notebook:

- **Data source.** Every file read from the iteration-7 workspaces (`dep(...)`) reads from the loaded `data` instead. `ROOT` is `eval_demo_run/`.
- **Runtime labels and hidden tests.** The runtime labels come from the stored trace of the sandboxed bench tracer, which is not shipped. The AST labels are recomputed live from the program text. The frozen v1 rules are the v2 rules with the one iteration-8 recogniser switched off; this was checked against the bench snapshot on every shipped program. The controls' hidden-test results are also stored.
- **Judges.** They are not called (no API key needed); their cached labels ship with the data.
- **Kth smallest only.** The demo carries kth smallest (T03) only:
  - the A5 placebo checks T03 (the original also checks shortest path and connected components);
  - `MANUAL` keeps the 4 kth-smallest manual reads;
  - the A4 agreement sample uses the kth-smallest part of the recorded `random.Random(7)` draw, which was made over all 162 programs;
  - item 0a omits its shortest-path pilot block.
- **Stages that need inputs not shipped.** `s0`, items 0b–0k, `apply` and `out` need the 1.3 MB report and six more iteration-7 artifacts. They are left as comments in `main()`. The `audit` stage runs headline checks H1–H2 of `audit/headline_check.py` in-process.

## Rebuilding the data

```bash
python3 -I build_mini_demo_data.py --artifact-dir <folder of gen_art_evaluation_7 (iteration 8)> \
    --dep-dir <folder holding the iteration-7 artifacts, e.g. gen_art_experiment_33>
```

## Restoring removed files

Nothing in this folder is marked `delete` in `.aii/manifest.yaml`, and nothing is 100 MB or larger. The notebook's
scratch output folder `eval_demo_run/` is not kept; running `code_demo.ipynb` recreates it in about 10 s.
`mini_demo_data.json` can be rebuilt with `build_mini_demo_data.py` (above).
````

The original has one more edit with two values, the shortest-path pilot, which needs\ndata the demo does not carry.\n\n## Layout\n\n| Path | What it is |\n|-|-|\n| `code_demo.ipynb` | the notebook: `eval.py` and the section modules stage `a` calls, split into cells with explanations; saved with its outputs (comparison table and a 6-panel figure) |\n| `mini_demo_data.json` | the demo data, one dataset `kth_smallest_T03_cells`: 82 exp33 arm cells (960 programs, program text for the 252 programs of the 11 cells relabelled live); the metadata holds exp33's kth-smallest config, its published pooled recovery, the labeller-validation inputs and the full run's numbers for comparison |\n| `build_mini_demo_data.py` | how `mini_demo_data.json` was built (stdlib only); it also checks that the shipped AST rules reproduce every stored AST label |\n| `.aii/manifest.yaml` | storage decisions (none needed: nothing here is heavy) |\n\nThe notebook loads `mini_demo_data.json` from this repository's raw GitHub URL and falls back to the local copy.\n\n## How to run\n\n**Colab:** open `code_demo.ipynb` and run all cells. The first cell installs `loguru` and nothing else; Colab already\nhas numpy and matplotlib. Runtime is about 10 s on a CPU.\n\n**Locally** (Python 3.12):\n\n```bash\nuv venv --seed --python 3.12 .nbenv\nuv pip install --python .nbenv/bin/python jupyter ipykernel\n.nbenv/bin/jupyter nbconvert --to notebook --execute code_demo.ipynb --output code_demo.ipynb\n```\n\nOff Colab, the install cell pins numpy 2.0.2 and matplotlib 3.10.0, Colab's versions. Running the notebook writes the\nsame intermediate files as the original into `eval_demo_run/`: `results/*.json`, `tables/*.csv`, `audit/*.json`,\n`corrections.json` and `logs/run.log`.\n\n**Configuration** (config cell):\n- `BOOT`: the number of slot-bootstrap resamples, 2000 as in exp33. The placebo matches exp33's intervals exactly only at 2000; with fewer it compares the point estimates.\n- `STAGES`: `a,audit`.\n\nScale-up record, clean-venv runs including package installs: `BOOT` = 100 took 10 s, 500 took 9 s, 2000 took 10–11 s.\n\n## What differs from the original `eval.py`\n\nThe code is the original code, split into cells. Only these changes were made, each marked in the notebook:\n\n- **Data source.** Every file read from the iteration-7 workspaces (`dep(...)`) reads from the loaded `data` instead. `ROOT` is `eval_demo_run/`.\n- **Runtime labels and hidden tests.** The runtime labels come from the stored trace of the sandboxed bench tracer, which is not shipped. The AST labels are recomputed live from the program text. The frozen v1 rules are the v2 rules with the one iteration-8 recogniser switched off; this was checked against the bench snapshot on every shipped program. The controls' hidden-test results are also stored.\n- **Judges.** They are not called (no API key needed); their cached labels ship with the data.\n- **Kth smallest only.** The demo carries kth smallest (T03) only:\n  - the A5 placebo checks T03 (the original also checks shortest path and connected components);\n  - `MANUAL` keeps the 4 kth-smallest manual reads;\n  - the A4 agreement sample uses the kth-smallest part of the recorded `random.Random(7)` draw, which was made over all 162 programs;\n  - item 0a omits its shortest-path pilot block.\n- **Stages that need inputs not shipped.** `s0`, items 0b–0k, `apply` and `out` need the 1.3 MB report and six more iteration-7 artifacts. They are left as comments in `main()`. The `audit` stage runs headline checks H1–H2 of `audit/headline_check.py` in-process.\n\n## Rebuilding the data\n\n```bash\npython3 -I build_mini_demo_data.py --artifact-dir <folder of gen_art_evaluation_7 (iteration 8)> \\\n    --dep-dir <folder holding the iteration-7 artifacts, e.g. gen_art_experiment_33>\n```\n\n## Restoring removed files\n\nNothing in this folder is marked `delete` in `.aii/manifest.yaml`, and nothing is 100 MB or larger. The notebook's\nscratch output folder `eval_demo_run/` is not kept; running `code_demo.ipynb` recreates it in about 10 s.\n`mini_demo_data.json` can be rebuilt with `build_mini_demo_data.py` (above).\n", "structuredPatch": [], "originalFile": null, "userModified": false}

### [289] ASSISTANT · 2026-10-26 13:44:44 UTC

```
I converted the record-repair `eval.py` into a demo notebook that runs end to end in a clean venv in about 10 s. At the original 2000 bootstrap resamples, all 24 numbers it recomputes match the original full run exactly.

`eval.py` itself only calls into section modules, and those read files from earlier experiments that won't exist on Colab. So the notebook copies in the module code that stage `a` uses and points its file reads at the demo data. It runs on all 960 real kth-smallest programs, using:
- **The labeller fix:** the extended labeller is validated first, then all 960 programs are relabelled, giving 35 changes, all `other` → counting sort.
- **The adjudication:** cached judge labels plus manual reads; the judges are not called, so no API key is needed.
- **The recompute:** exp33's own recovery and bootstrap code.
- **The ledger and headline check:** the item 0a values and report edits, then checks H1–H2 of the independent headline check.

**What the notebook reproduces at 2000 resamples:**
- **Old numbers:** exp33's published recovery and confidence intervals, exactly.
- **Corrected recovery:** 1.59 / 1.57 / 1.55 becomes 1.01 / 1.00 / 0.99 at DAS ranks 1 / 4 / 16.
- **Ledger and edits:** the 16 report edits and 22 ledger values match the original run's text exactly. The original has one more edit (a shortest-path table) that needs data not shipped here.
- **Scaling:** 100 → 500 → 2000 resamples took 10 s, 9 s and 11 s in a clean venv.

**What it doesn't run:** stage `s0`, items 0b–0k, `apply`, `out` and the full re-derivation audit. They need the 1.3 MB report and six other experiment folders; the notebook says so where each one would run.

Departures from the original code, each marked in the notebook:
- Runtime labels come from the stored sandbox traces; the AST labels are recomputed live.
- Only kth-smallest data is shipped, so the placebo checks only that task.
- Only the 4 kth-smallest manual reads are kept.
- The agreement sample uses the recorded draw.

Process notes:
- **No todo tool:** this session had no todo-tracking tool, so I tracked TODOs 1–6 myself.
- **NotebookEdit:** it created all 45 cells. After the first test run the notebook grew too large for the read that NotebookEdit requires first. So the last few small edits (figure tweaks and the resample count) were exact JSON edits by cell ID.
- **Read-only folder:** an early check imported the artifact's labeller and wrote 4 `.pyc` files into that folder. I deleted them straight away and stopped the builder writing bytecode; no files there differ now, only directory timestamps.
- **Data URL:** it returns 404 until the push, so the notebook currently uses the local data file.

I treated the research brief at the end of the paste as background for this artifact and did not start new experiments.

Files are in the workspace:
- `code_demo.ipynb`
- `mini_demo_data.json` — one dataset: 82 arm cells, 960 programs, 457 KB
- `build_mini_demo_data.py` — rebuilds the data file
- `README.md`
- `.aii/manifest.yaml` — no entries; nothing is large
- `.terminal_claude_agent_struct_out.json`
```
